# Deploys & Rollback

How production deploys and rollbacks work for BoldLine OS.

## How it deploys
- Production = the **`main`** branch. Netlify auto-builds and deploys whenever `main`
  changes.
- All work happens on `claude/zen-babbage-1wtcv3`, then gets **auto-merged into `main`**
  after each completed unit of work (Bryson's standing instruction, 2026-06-29).

## The safety net (so we can always roll back)
Three independent ways back to the last working version:

1. **Netlify deploy history** — Netlify keeps every past deploy and can re-publish any
   of them instantly, no code changes. This is the fastest path.
2. **`rollback/<timestamp>` branches** — right before every merge, the current production
   state of `main` is saved to a branch named `rollback/<UTC timestamp>` and pushed.
   Each is a frozen snapshot of production *before* that merge. They accumulate on
   purpose (each = one saved restore point).
   - List them, newest last: `git branch -r --list 'origin/rollback/*'`
3. **The merge commit's first parent** — every deploy merge is a `--no-ff` merge commit,
   so its first parent is always the exact pre-merge state, recoverable via `revert`.

> Note: this git remote **blocks tag pushes and branch deletions**, so restore points
> are pushed *branches* (not tags), and old `rollback/*` branches can't be deleted —
> that's fine, we want them kept. (A leftover `zz-rollback-test` branch from setup is
> harmless and can't be removed.)

## How to roll back

### Fastest (no code) — Netlify
1. Netlify dashboard → the site → **Deploys**.
2. Find the last deploy that worked (each row shows commit + time).
3. Click it → **Publish deploy**. Production reverts immediately.

### Code-level — git revert (clean, preferred)
Back out the most recent deploy merge and let Netlify redeploy the prior code:
```
git checkout main && git pull origin main
git revert -m 1 <merge-commit-sha>     # the "Merge … into main (deploy)" commit
git push origin main
```

### Restore to a specific saved branch
```
git checkout main && git pull origin main
git reset --hard origin/rollback/<timestamp>
git push --force-with-lease origin main
```
Prefer the revert or Netlify route; force-push rewrites `main`.

In-area alerts **name the matched city** so the claim is checkable; out-of-area alerts pass through explicitly flagged **"NOT this client's area"** so they inform seasonal wording without becoming a false claim. **Autopilot** fetches lazily and at most once per client per run (a run writing no challengers makes zero network calls), with `localCond` scoped **inside the client loop** so a Phoenix heat warning cannot leak into a Seattle client's ad. **Every failure path returns season-only context** — network down, non-200, timeout, unexpected JSON — because an ad that ships without a weather angle is fine and one that never ships is not. **`tests/verify-local-conditions.mjs`, 91 checks, no network** (alerts stubbed, so it cannot flake on real weather): all 18 excluded event types refused, all 15 usable ones accepted, exact-place matching both ways, the four graceful-failure paths, and that each of the four writers really imports it, calls it and puts it in the prompt. **Both critical guards proved to fail** — removing wildfire from the exclusion list gave 5 failures, dropping conditions from the autopilot prompt gave 1 — then restored. **Not built, deliberately:** forecast temperatures (needs geocoding), a conditions-CHANGE rewrite trigger (fingerprint is recorded and ready, but firing on every advisory posting and expiring would churn live ads, so it needs a minimum-dwell rule first), and non-weather local events (needs a search API, high irrelevance rate). Watches and warnings are issued by county, so this was **most of the severe weather**, and it suppressed precisely the angle he was asking for. It hit live alerts too, not only the new window. County zones are now recognised by shape, and the client's own towns are resolved to their county through OpenStreetMap Nominatim (free, no key, cached, and only called when a county-issued alert actually appears). **It fails soft in a specific direction:** an unresolvable county is reported as **UNCONFIRMED**, never as "elsewhere", because "elsewhere" was the false statement. So a location outside America **could never resolve**, and a client based in one state could not advertise into another country at all. **Google also pinned `languageConstants/1000` (English) as the only targeted language**, which is right for a Phoenix roofer and silently wrong the moment anyone advertises into Mexico, Quebec or Japan: the ads simply never show to most of the market, and nothing reports it. **`netlify/lib/geo-parse.mjs` is SHARED on purpose** — two separate country parsers would drift, and a drift here means advertising in the wrong country, which nobody notices until the money is gone. It reads the country off the END of each entry so one box handles everything: `"Gilbert, Arizona"` searches the US with the state kept (it is what tells Phoenix, Arizona from Phoenix, Oregon), `"London, United Kingdom"` searches GB with the country **stripped from the query** (both platforms already search inside a country, so repeating it only hurts the match), `"Toronto, Ontario"` is recognised as Canadian without the country being typed, and `"Canada"` targets the whole country with no lookup at all. **🔴 IT NEVER GUESSES SILENTLY.** An entry whose country cannot be determined falls back to the default **and is returned as `assumed`**, so combined with the existing rule that an unresolvable location is fatal, a mistyped place can neither be silently dropped nor quietly advertised in the wrong country. **Languages are RESOLVED, not hardcoded** — `resolveLanguages()` looks names or codes up against Google's own `language_constant` table, defaults to English (what every existing campaign already targeted) and accepts `"all"` to remove language targeting. The ids are deliberately not hardcoded: **guessing a language constant id and getting it wrong targets the wrong audience exactly as quietly as a wrong geo id**, so it follows the same resolve-don't-guess rule. Meta's two-letter Country box is now labelled **"If unclear"** and is only the fallback for a line naming no country of its own. **PARITY AUDIT.** Every server-side `internal` branch was checked and they are **correct by design** — BoldLine does not email, nurture, invoice, report to or run lead-follow-up on itself. **One real gap, now closed:** the house account could always type a NICHE and rebuild, so BoldLine runs one campaign per kind of business it chases; a client had **no equivalent**, and needs one for the same structural reason — *one ad group holding "roof repair" and "roof replacement" can never match either search well*. Both launch cards now ask a client **"Which service is this campaign for?"**, seed the name, copy and keywords from it, and **pass it into the AI brief as the niche and the offer** — without that last part the model writes for the whole trade while the campaign name and seeds say one service, and the two disagree. **One deliberate exception kept:** Meta split testing stays house-only, because that is the `META-TIER-GATE` (Meta's Development tier only permits writes to owned accounts, so removing it would have Meta rejecting writes every two hours forever); **Google creative testing has no such restriction and already ran for clients**. Everything else from this week already reached clients: both conditions windows, the conditions card, the creative strategy switch, the delivery-chain activation and the geo guards. **`verify-campaign-launch` now 104 checks**, five more deliberate breaks confirmed to fail (Google reverting to a hardcoded country, language pinned back to English, the parser marking an unplaceable entry as confidently placed, the per-service box hidden from clients, and the client's service not reaching the AI). **🔴 A test failed on a character-window proxy for the SECOND time** — `verify-local-conditions` asserted the Meta generator sends its service area *"within 900 characters of `action:"meta"`"* and a comment added to that call pushed it out of range while the wiring stayed perfectly correct. Same trap as the 400-character proxy already recorded in `repo-tests`. It now slices the call and asserts the field is inside it, re-confirmed to catch the real regression. Service worker → **v15**. KB: `campaign-launch-bugs`. |

| 2026-08-20 | `6d96fee` | `rollback/20260820-194716` | `e8a5e59` | **Meta ads now optimise for ARRIVALS, not taps.** Spotted in Meta's own ad-set editor while Bryson checked his first campaign, before it had spent a cent. The ad set's `optimization_goal` was **`LINK_CLICKS`**; it is now **`LANDING_PAGE_VIEWS`**. **The difference is what you pay for.** A link click counts the tap. A landing page view counts the tap *and* the page actually loading. The gap between them is mis-taps and people who swiped away on a slow connection before anything rendered — and under `LINK_CLICKS` Meta optimises to produce **taps**, so it goes looking for precisely the people most likely to tap and least likely to arrive, and every one of them is billed. **Why it was LINK_CLICKS originally and why that reasoning expired:** it was chosen because `LANDING_PAGE_VIEWS` used to require a pixel and there was none. Both halves have since changed — Meta now serves the goal **without** a pixel (it says so in the editor) and BoldLine's pixel `2164699294444030` is live and firing on `/get-started`. `billing_event` deliberately stays `IMPRESSIONS`: that is what Meta *charges* on, not what it hunts for. **Timing matters** — switching the goal after a campaign has spent resets Meta's learning phase, so the free moment is while it still reads "Scheduled" at $0.00. Applies to every campaign the OS builds, house and client alike. Also recorded, because it looks exactly like a broken launch and is not: **a freshly published Meta campaign reads "Scheduled" rather than "Active" with $0.00 spent**, because `start_time` is deliberately set **one hour ahead** so nothing can begin spending the instant it is created, before anyone has looked at it. **108 checks in `verify-campaign-launch.mjs`, break confirmed to fail.** Separately, the intermittent `boldlinemedia.com` timeouts flagged in the previous entry were **NOT a real fault** — Bryson confirmed the page loads fine from his phone, and the sandbox's own egress to Netlify's apex IP was the cause. | 2026-08-22 | `85781c5` | `rollback/20260822T004428Z` | `fbb2cfb` | **"The delete doesn't work" — two defects, one of them an hour old.** Bryson, minutes after the house-account mirror shipped: *"the test leads popped up and I deleted them but every time I reload the app they are still there so the delete doesnt work."* **🔴 (1) A CALENDLY LEAD IS RE-DERIVED, SO DELETING THE ROW CANNOT REMOVE IT.** The Calendly sync runs every 15 minutes over a 14-day window and asks, per booking, *"is there already a lead carrying this event id?"*. Deleting the row answered **no**, so the next run created it again. **The delete worked perfectly every single time** — the lead was rebuilt within the quarter hour, which is indistinguishable from a delete that never fired. A website form submission cannot come back this way (pushed once, never re-read), which is why only bookings behaved like this. This is the general hazard with **any** poller that re-derives records from an external source: **deletion is not durable unless something remembers the deletion.** The fix is a **tombstone, and it is the row itself** — every personal field is cleared (`name`, `email`, `business`, `recommended`, `notes`) and the row is re-filed as `form:"deleted"`, keeping nothing but the opaque event id. It vanishes from the Leads screen and from the house account (both filter that form) and the sync's existing lookup finds the id and skips the booking permanently, with **no change to the sync**, because its dedupe queries the whole table without filtering on form. Non-Calendly leads are still hard-deleted. **🔴 WHY NOT A SEPARATE TOMBSTONE ROW, which was the first design:** `website_leads` RLS grants the signed-in app **SELECT, UPDATE and DELETE but NOT INSERT** (only the marketing site's service-role key inserts, bypassing RLS), so a marker row would have been **refused every time**. An update is permitted, and re-using the row is better anyway since it removes the person's details rather than keeping a second copy of them. **🔴 (2) NEWSLETTER SUBSCRIBERS WERE BEING MIRRORED AS LEADS — my own bug, one hour old.** The mirror selected **every** row in `website_leads`, and that table also holds the durable backup of the Resend subscriber list, which the Leads screen has always filtered out. So every subscriber was landing on the house account as a lead, **inflating both the lead count and the cost-per-lead divided by it** — a wrong number presented as real, which is worse than the zero it replaced. Filtering is a **denylist, not an allowlist**: enquiry rows are named after whichever Netlify form they came from, so a form added later must arrive as a lead by default rather than silently vanish. Rows mirrored before the filter existed prune themselves on the next run. **One ordering detail is load-bearing:** the prune gate measures the **raw** page length, before filtering — filtering first would make a full page look short and re-enable pruning on an incomplete read, the exact data loss the gate exists to prevent. **(3) A REFUSED WRITE NO LONGER HIDES.** The lead save and lead delete previously reported failure to the browser console only while the screen carried on as if it had worked; both now show a plain message, and a refused delete **reloads the list** so what is displayed matches what is stored. **73 checks (was 49), seven more deliberate breaks confirmed to fail.** Service worker → **v26**. KB: `house-leads-mirror`. |
| 2026-08-22 | `98744b1` | `rollback/20260822T013557Z` | `759edb3` | **The bot chat's message box was never missing, the bottom nav was painting over it.** Bryson: *"when I try to chat with the bots I can't see the message box."* The giveaway was in his screenshot: the page behind the sheet was dimmed by the overlay while **the nav was not**, so the nav was above the overlay rather than below it. **🔴 A Z-INDEX THAT COULD NOT COMPETE.** `BottomNav` is `z-index:50` and `Sheet` is `z-index:80`, so on paper the sheet wins. It did not, because the screen wrapper `.os-content` runs `animation: osIn .35s ease both`, and **an element animating opacity forms its own stacking context** which `fill-mode: both` keeps filling forever after the animation ends. Every z-index inside it is then only comparable **within** it, and the wrapper itself sits at the default level, so a sibling at 50 painted over the whole thing regardless of the number inside. **Raising the z-index would not have helped; the number was never the problem.** **This is why only the BOT sheet was affected** — the notification, ARIA and More sheets render as siblings of the nav at the top level, while the bot sheet opens from inside the client hub, deep inside `.os-content`. **Same component, different ancestor, opposite result**, which is what makes this class of bug nearly impossible to spot by reading the component. Two centred modals at `z-index:400` carried the same latent flaw unnoticed, because their content sits mid-screen where the nav does not reach. **Fixed twice on purpose:** `.os-content` now uses `backwards` rather than `both` (still fades in, still no first-frame flash, but releases the context when it ends, which also rescues those modals), and **`Sheet` portals to `<body>`** so a sheet is outside every screen's stacking context wherever it is opened from. The app shell sets the typeface on itself rather than on `<body>`, so the portalled overlay carries `APP_FONT` and the text colour explicitly or it would render in the browser's default serif. Separately, **`ChatBar` never cleared the phone's home indicator** — pinned Save bars already had the safe-area rule and the chat bar was simply missed on a flat 20px. **MEASURED, NOT ASSERTED:** the new suite builds the real structure in a headless browser and reads `elementFromPoint` inside the nav's band **both ways** — with `both` the nav is on top (the bug, reproduced), with `backwards` the overlay is (the fix). A regex could not have told a real fix from a plausible-looking edit. **🔴 AND A NEAR MISS WORTH RECORDING:** the first draft of the code comment explaining this fix wrapped `backwards` and `both` in backticks. The app's entire stylesheet lives inside a JSX template literal, so **one backtick in a CSS comment ends the string early and the whole file fails to parse** — not a broken style, a blank OS. Caught by running the file through Babel before merging; there is now a dependency-free assertion that the style block contains no backtick. `index.html` is one ~1MB Babel block, so any syntax error takes the entire app down: parse it before merging anything that touches it. **19 checks, six deliberate breaks confirmed to fail.** Service worker → **v27**. KB: `sheet-layering`. |

| 2026-09-02 | `f1b5d70` | `rollback/20260902T014805Z` | `e94edfc` | **The reveal keeps happening, and the hover lifts actually lift.** Bryson: *"make sure the micro animations such as a scroll and clicking, giving, etc. always happen (this is mainly for scrolling to make sure the up and down animation happens even after a person has scrolled through the whole page)"*. 🔴 **He was right and it was worse than he thought: MOST OF THE PAGE NEVER ANIMATED AT ALL.** Two things combined. The observer called `unobserve` the first time an element appeared, so nothing could ever play twice; and the 1.5s safety net revealed EVERY element on the page whether it had been reached or not. So on a real visit everything below the first screenful was marked revealed before the visitor got near it and just sat there. The scroll reveal had effectively only ever worked for the hero. **The net cannot simply be deleted** — it is the only thing keeping the original blank-sections bug out of contexts nobody scrolls (the preview iframe, a screenshot, a visitor who never moves). So the fix is four pieces, one per failure mode: a REVEAL observer that never un-observes; a RE-ARM observer at 160px whose region strictly contains the reveal observer's, so the two can never fight at the boundary; the SAFETY NET, unchanged in what it guarantees; and FIRST SCROLL, which cancels the net and re-arms what it already revealed. **Without that fourth piece there are two broken visitors** — one who scrolls before 1.5s (the net fires anyway and reveals everything below) and the slow reader who scrolls after it (the net has run and the re-arm observer will never fire again, because those elements' intersection state never changed). 🔴 **SECOND BUG, FOUND THE SAME WAY: EVERY HOVER LIFT ON THE PAGE WAS DEAD ON ARRIVAL.** Cards, chips, steps and review tiles are all `.reveal`, and `.js .reveal.in` outranks `.bcard:hover` on specificity, so it pinned `transform:none` and nothing moved. The shadow and border halves of each hover rule DID work, which is exactly why it looked plausible. Fixed by moving the reveal onto the `translate` property, which the browser composes with `transform` rather than competing with it: the reveal owns `translate`, every hover and press owns `transform`. Side benefit, `--rv` is now a bare Y distance spent on `translate:0 var(--rv)`, so a sideways reveal is **structurally impossible** rather than merely absent. **Neither bug is visible in the source** — both read as correct CSS and correct JS — so the audit now DRIVES the page: a replay check (scroll to a section, away, back; it must animate every time, on both the early-scroller and slow-reader paths) and a hover check (hovering must actually change the computed transform on `.bcard/.step/.rev/.chip`, plus the press on the main button, which is all a phone visitor gets). Its main sweep reads DOWN the page a screen at a time rather than measuring once from the top, since an element far below the fold is now supposed to be armed and hidden. 🔴 **One mutation escaped for a timing reason:** removing `clearTimeout(net)` produced no failure, because the replay check reached the section at ~1s, before the net fired at 1.5s, so it passed for the wrong reason. It now sits near the top until the net has had its chance and then asks whether the far-below section was revealed behind the visitor's back. All four mutations caught after that. Static side: `verify-landing-motion.mjs` at 154 checks. `AUDIT_ONLY=behaviour` skips the 144-load sweep when bisecting. 🔴 Fifth time a backtick inside the `css` template literal killed the renderer; the suite catches it, a one-off probe does not. KB: `landing-page-issues`. |

So one switch would have fired the ENTIRE backlog in a single run: several emails at once, each announcing a post that went live weeks ago as though it were new, and that is the first thing a brand new subscriber would ever have received. **The fix is a rule rather than a one-off tidy-up.** `STALE_AFTER_HOURS = 48`: an email more than 48h past its send time is RETIRED, not sent, and that happens **whether or not sending is on** — so the existing pile clears itself on the next hourly run, before he ever flips the switch, and the same trap is not re-armed the next time sending is paused. The 2026-08-28 plan records that the reviewer wanted live impressions, clicks, reach, spend **and conversions**, and that *"one column is still empty, and it is the one the reviewer named: CONVERSIONS"*, which is why resubmission moved from 4 Sep to **10 Sep**. A page-views campaign can never fill it; the API-volume half is fed by `ads-sync` hourly and is unaffected. **Built:** `objective` is `OUTCOME_LEADS` + `OFFSITE_CONVERSIONS` + `promoted_object {pixel_id, LEAD}` when a pixel id is supplied, else unchanged. `destination_type: "WEBSITE"` is explicit, **or Meta may serve an on-Facebook instant form** that bypasses the landing page, the lead pipeline and the CRM forward. 🔴 **THE SWITCH IS A PASTED PIXEL ID, NEVER DETECTED** (new `metaPixelId` field): a client without one gets the campaign REJECTED at creation, and worse, optimising for a LEAD event on a pixel that never fires one makes Meta under-deliver and buy nothing, which is quieter than a rejection. Now wrapped in `withFailureAlert`, with each swallowed error raising a red alert naming the step. | 2026-09-02 | `779c9a4` | `rollback/20260902T201734Z` | `e001d80` | **The My Ads campaign card was dead the moment he opened it, and the whole bug class is now caught statically.** Bryson: *"this came up when i pressed campaign under my ads"* — `Cannot access 'metaLocations' before initialization`. Two things combined: **`useState({...})` takes a PLAIN OBJECT, not a lazy initialiser**, so it is built on the very first render and only LOOKS deferred; and **`const` is not hoisted the way `var` is**, so a `var` would have been `undefined` and limped along while a `const` throws and React unmounts the card. Declaration moved above the `useState`, with a comment saying anything else that `useState` reads has to move up too. 🔴 **THE REASON THIS SECTION EXISTS AT ALL: every single guard was blind to it, because it is VALID JAVASCRIPT.** Babel compiled it, the app booted, all forty suites were green, and production was broken — the identical shape to the `useMemo` bug that created `verify-app-boots.mjs` in the first place. A syntax check cannot see it; it only throws when that one component renders, and only he renders it. So the suite now parses the shipping `<script type="text/babel">` block with `@babel/parser`, walks every scope with `@babel/traverse`, and fails on any `const`/`let` referenced textually before its declaration in the same run of statements. **A reference that crosses a function boundary is exempt** (that code runs later), which is what keeps the rule usable rather than something the codebase has to be bent around: against the fixed 1.1MB app it reports **zero** hits. Broken once, as required — pointed at the pre-fix file it named `metaLocations` on line 8577 declared on 8605, and nothing else. `verify-app-boots` 24 → 26 checks; full suite green. So completeness had to be asked as its own question of the FINAL text: `isLeadIn()` rejects a kicker ending on a verb still waiting for its object, or on a dangling article or preposition, and replaces it with the niche label ("For Roofers") or drops it when no niche is known, because the canvas draws nothing for an empty kicker and nothing reads as deliberate where half a thought reads as broken. 🔴 **The question-word exemption is load-bearing and is pinned as its own check**: "What Your Budget Buys" and "Where Your Money Goes" end on verbs in the reject list and are good copy, so a label opening on what/how/why/where/when/who/which is exempt. It also keeps an attorney review of the base document meaningful, since the base never moves. **Pinned by rendering the contract with and without terms and asserting the rest of the document is BYTE-IDENTICAL.** 🔴 **Nothing saves itself**: the function reads the record to check for a signature and never writes to it, and a check fails if it ever does. Clause text is escaped, and the check that matters is not the script tag but the clause that **closes the paragraph and opens a forged section heading**, making the document appear to say something it does not. High-risk topics (liability, governing law, IP, ad spend, termination, exclusivity) are **flagged, not blocked** — he is entitled to negotiate any of them and a tool that refuses to write what he agreed is one he stops using — so the warning lands on the clause that deserves a second read. The prompt refuses outright only on the hard business constraint: no clause may ever have BoldLine fronting, holding or being billed for a client's ad spend. **16 checks running BOTH real copies of the contract generator; nine mutations, all caught.** 🔴 Two of those first failed on the two-copies DRIFT check rather than their own assertion, because only the server copy was mutated; re-run against both copies they failed correctly, which is the only way to know the specific guard works. | 2026-09-02 | `24be29c` | `rollback/20260903T035922Z` | `9f0e721` | **A client who goes quiet can now lose the contract and still owe money, and contracts are VERSIONED so no signed agreement ever changes.** Bryson: *"make sure in the contract from now on say something like if a client fails to respond, complete intake form, etc. the contract is voided and there is a fee charged as well (this will be for future clients not Sebastian until he renews his contract)"*. New section 6: intake and access within **14 days**, a **written reminder** if not, **10 days** after that, then BoldLine may end it immediately; the setup fee is earned and non-refundable and the Monthly Minimum stays payable for each month or part month already run. 🔴 **The money is framed as amounts EARNED plus RESERVED CAPACITY, never as a penalty, and that framing is the difference between a clause that holds and one a court strikes out**: a flat punishment for walking away is unenforceable in most US states unless it is a genuine estimate of loss, so the setup fee pays for work done and the minimum reflects a slot held open while other work was turned away. Both are real losses. It does **not** fire when the delay is BoldLine's or a platform's, which would be indefensible read aloud, and ending the agreement is **optional** so a slow client can simply be waited for. The one-time hand-off gets its own wording, since it has no Monthly Minimum to refer to. 🔴 **THE HARD HALF WAS "NOT SEBASTIAN", AND IT IS NOT A PREFERENCE.** This contract renders **fresh every time anyone opens it**, in the OS and in the client portal, so adding a section unconditionally would put a new obligation **with money attached** into an agreement a client has already signed. Retroactively. Silently. He would open his portal one day and find a clause he never agreed to, and the only way anyone would notice is if it were enforced. So terms are **versioned**: v1 is anything signed before 3 Sep 2026 (today, exactly one client), v2 adds this clause; unsigned means current terms, and an explicit `contractTermsVersion` on the record beats the date guess in both directions. 🔴 **Renewal STAMPS the current version, and the inference alone could never do it**: renewing sets `contractSigned` again but leaves `contractSignedAt` on the ORIGINAL signature date, so without the stamp an old client stays on old terms for ever, which is not what "until he renews" means. A signed contract with no date at all gets CURRENT terms, deliberately: an unknown date is far likelier to be a new record than a lost v1 client, and guessing v1 would silently drop the clause from every new agreement whose date failed to save. The section slots in **mid-document** so everything after it shifts, and a check asserts the numbering reads **1..N with no gap and no repeat** across every package and both versions, because a duplicated section number is exactly what a dispute turns on. **15 checks running BOTH real copies of the generator; six mutations, all caught.** 🔴 One mutation silently failed to apply and reported a pass; re-landed properly it failed correctly. A mutation that does not reach the code proves nothing, and reads identically to a working guard. KB: `contract-terms-versioning` (new). |
| 2026-09-02 | `a4014cc` | `rollback/20260903T041234Z` | `63a2a6e` | **Cancelling early now costs the REMAINING TERM, not one month (terms v3).** Bryson: *"a clause if the client wants to cancel... they have to tell me in writing and then pay the remaining months minimum and the set up fee is non refundable"*. 🔴 **Checked before building: written notice and 30 days were ALREADY in the agreement**, so the only thing that actually changed is the amount, from a one-month early-termination fee to every remaining month of the Committed Term (any part month counting as a whole one), plus the existing discount clawback, plus the Setup Fee now stated as non-refundable in any amount. Notice must go to **the email address on page one and takes effect when received**, because where notice went is the first thing argued about. 🔴 **HIS OWN QUESTION ANSWERED: their own minimum, never a flat $400 for a final part month.** A flat figure bearing no relation to their contract is precisely the shape a court reads as a **penalty** rather than a genuine estimate of loss, which would put the whole clause at risk; and on every package priced above it, a $400 tail would make cancelling in the last month **cheaper than seeing it out**, rewarding the exact behaviour the clause exists to discourage. The liquidated-damages framing from the old clause is kept and extended (reserved capacity for the whole term, other work turned away), because that framing is the only reason an acceleration like this survives a challenge. Gated as **v3**, so nobody who signed under earlier terms gets the harsher clause and the old wording stays in their document. 🔴 **A REAL BUG FOUND WHILE BUILDING IT, AND IT IS THE MOST INSTRUCTIVE PART: the unsigned default was written as a literal `2`.** Adding v3 therefore left **every new client silently on v2** — the clause was in the file, gated correctly, rendered nowhere, and nothing failed or looked wrong. It now reads one `TERMS_CURRENT`, and a check fails if a literal returns. 🔴 **Two EXISTING assertions updated rather than deleted**: both matched the phrase `early-termination fee equal to`, so the new wording made them report the fee as **GONE when it had only got bigger**. | 2026-09-04 | (see next row) | | | 🔴 **THE OS LOADED TO A SILENT WHITE SCREEN ON HIS PHONE, AND THE APP WAS FINE.** Bryson: *"the os won't load it just keeps going to a white screen"*. An hour went into hunting a bug that did not exist. 🔴 **THE FAILURE HAD NO REPORTER, WHICH IS THE WHOLE STORY.** Three safety nets existed and not one could fire: `window.onerror` never ran because **nothing threw**; there was no React error boundary and one could not have helped because **React was the missing piece**; and the service worker cached the app shell while waving **every** cross-origin request straight through. React, ReactDOM, Babel and Supabase all load from **unpkg**, so one dropped bar of signal means the babel-compiled block is never transformed — **because Babel is the thing that transforms it** — nothing runs, nothing throws, the page is blank forever, and the person looking at it cannot tell anybody anything. 🔴 **THE TRANSFERABLE LESSON: an error handler only catches errors.** The worst failures do not raise one, they just fail to happen, and nothing that waits to be told will ever notice. **Ruled out first, and the method is worth keeping:** the app compiles and mounts (headlessly, with **LOCAL** React/Babel — 🔴 the sandbox reaches no CDN, so a blank page in a naive harness proves NOTHING, a mistake made once here and corrected mid-diagnosis); it renders signed in with realistic data on the exact screen he was on; `curl` showed the deploy fully landed; the page grew 7 KB all day; and there is no `localStorage`, so nothing reopens a broken screen. **THREE FIXES.** The service worker now caches unpkg **cache-first** (`CACHE_VERSION` → **v71**, or installed apps keep the old worker and none of it activates) — pinned URLs that never change, so nothing goes stale, and it leaves the critical path; everything else stays network-first so a deploy never is; and an error response is never cached, because a cached 404 for a library breaks the app permanently. A **plain-DOM check** that the libraries arrived now says *"The OS could not finish loading... almost always the connection rather than the OS. Nothing is lost."* with a Try again button and the list of what is missing — 🔴 it uses **no library and sits outside the babel block**, because everything it might depend on is exactly what has gone missing, and it never paints over a root that already rendered. And a React **error boundary** turns a render crash into a readable message with **Reload**, **Clear and reload** (unregisters the worker and wipes the caches, the only thing that frees a phone holding a half-loaded app) and **Copy details**, shown on screen rather than logged to a console he does not have. **13 checks, and both paths reproduced in a real browser** — blocking unpkg gives the blank page before and the explanation after; a deliberate throw confirms the boundary. 🔴 Two existing suites broke and were fixed rather than loosened: a comment containing the literal babel script tag made them find the app's code **twice**, and `verify-portal-preview` evaluates the OS against a hand-written React stub where `class extends React.Component` throws at DEFINITION time. KB: `white-screen`. |

Each row is a button now; pressing it drives the five tiles, pressing again or **Show all** returns to the account total, and the header always names what is on screen. 🔴 **THE CARD HAD TO BECOME A REAL COMPONENT FIRST.** It was an inline arrow inside `ClientHub` called from a `&&`, and **a hook cannot live there**: the day the condition flips the hook order changes and React tears the screen down. 🔴 **THE FAKE COST PER LEAD IS THE TRAP WORTH REMEMBERING.** The account tiles read leads from `leadsLog`, every enquiry the business received from anywhere; **a single campaign has no such number**, and pairing it with one campaign's spend yields a cost per lead that is **pure fiction and looks entirely plausible** — the worst kind of wrong number. A focused campaign therefore reports the **platform's own conversion count** and the tile is **RELABELLED** ("Conversions, counted by Google" / "Leads, counted by Meta") rather than quietly changing what "Leads" means. Account-wide notes are hidden while one campaign is focused, because beside a single campaign they answer a question nobody asked. **And the other half of his message, "only the one from before is showing numbers": nothing is broken.** `ads-sync` runs **hourly** and both platforms report over the **last 30 days**, so a campaign live for an hour genuinely has almost nothing — the card now says *"Running, but nobody has seen it yet... these figures refresh once an hour"* instead of showing five bare zeros, and a paused one says its zeros are because it has not run. **22 checks; ten mutations, all caught, plus a real-browser run that presses a campaign and confirms the top tile shows THAT campaign's 4,360 views rather than the account's 5,260.** 🔴 One mutation escaped first: `/Show all/` passed with the button behind `false&&` because the string was still in the file — **checking a line EXISTS is not checking it is SHOWN**, re-pinned on the render gate. 🔴 Two existing checks broke because they pinned the card's inline arrow rather than the rule, and `verify-house-leads` reported "clients lost the card" when it had merely been extracted; **a pattern that pins a signature pins more than it means to**. KB: `campaign-breakdown`. |

| 2026-09-04 | (see next row) | | | **The monthly OS report stopped counting BoldLine as a client and started reporting the month, and the last client-readable em dashes are gone.** Bryson: *"make sure it takes information from the whole month and uses it not just what it sees when it writes the report"* and *"it is also saying i have two clients but i only have 1 and then my own ads running"*. 🔴 **THE CLIENT COUNT**: BoldLine's own ad account is a row in `clients` with `internal: true`, because that is how the OS gives its own ads the same machinery every client gets. Each rewritten into real sentences rather than find-and-replaced, and the file is now scanned; 🔴 **the entity form counts too**, since the footer read `&mdash;` and would have survived a search for the character. 🔴 **And the package name, which had a carve-out declaring it exempt.** Renaming it exposed that **the marketing site and the catalog never agreed**: the site said "Full System: Growth", the OS said "Full System — Growth", and a normaliser in `verify-packages` quietly folded the two together, so **a client could read one name on the public site and a different one in their own portal**. Both now use the site's colon form, which keeps the public name and the Meta flip checklist intact and makes them agree for the first time. **Both exemptions deleted**, so the guarantee is stronger than before rather than differently shaped. | 2026-09-04 | (see next row) | | | **Saved copies of a client's landing page, and the contract right to publish them.** Bryson: *"have aria take screenshots of published landing pages... that way we can use it for content and case studies"*, *"make sure there is a way for me to access the saved page and delete them when I want"*, and *"should we also add in future contracts that we are able to use any work we've done for content purposes... but will not sell information"*. 🔴 **THE SCREENSHOT IS THE WEAKER HALF. THERE IS NO COPY OF A LANDING PAGE AT ALL.** `landing.mjs` rebuilds every page from Supabase on every single request, which is why an edit is live instantly with no publish step. It also means **the version that produced leads exists only until somebody edits the record** — a changed headline, a cleared account, and it is gone, not archived, not recoverable. A screenshot is a low-resolution picture of that; the page is the thing and an image can be made from it later. New **Saved Copies of the Page** card under the landing page: Save, View, Delete, stored in a public bucket and listed on the record. 🔴 **AN ARCHIVE IS A PREVIEW, AND THIS IS THE SHARPEST CASE OF THAT RULE IN THE CODEBASE.** A landing page is **not inert**: it carries a submit handler pointing at `lead-intake?token=<the client's REAL lead token>`. Save it verbatim, open it months later to show somebody, tap the form, and **a REAL LEAD lands on that client's record and is forwarded to their CRM** from a page nobody is running — and the file sits in a **PUBLIC bucket**. So it is neutralised **when WRITTEN, not when shown**, because neutralising on display leaves the dangerous version in storage one direct link from being live: every script and noscript, every inline `on*` attribute, the form's `action`/`method` plus `onsubmit="return false"` plus a disabled fieldset, **any `lead-intake?token=` left in the text** (the token lived inside a script, but one unmatched script tag is all it takes), and every `href` parked as `data-archived-href` so a booking link cannot book and a phone link cannot ring. **The visual record is untouched** — only plumbing is cut, which is the whole point of saving the page rather than a picture — and a banner says it is a saved copy, because it is otherwise pixel-identical to the live site. 🔴 **It opens in a NEW TAB, never an iframe**, since it carries the client's own full-page styling; recorded in `verify-preview-safety`'s manifest comment so its absence there reads as a decision. **Deleting removes the FILE BEFORE the record**, because the reverse strands a public page nothing points at, and a failed save removes its own orphan. **CONTRACT TERMS v4**: v1-v3 carried one vague "portfolio rights" line, too thin to publish case studies on. v4 grants reproduction of the pages, ads, creative and campaign structure plus name and logo; 🔴 **promises explicitly that lead and customer information is never published, shared, licensed or sold**, naming names, emails, phone numbers and message contents, **which is not a courtesy but the thing that makes the rest signable**; excludes their costs, margins and supplier terms; allows results to be published generically on request; and pulls any single item within 30 days. `showcaseOptOut` swaps the whole grant for a refusal so a client saying no never costs the deal. A v3 signer never gains it, which is why terms are versioned. **26 checks; ten mutations, all caught.** 🔴 Three needed fixing first: removing the token scrub ESCAPED because the token happens to live inside a script today, so it is now exercised on markup where the strip cannot help; and `/window.confirm/` passed with the call behind `false&&`, the same checking-a-line-exists-is-not-checking-it-runs trap. **Not built: images or video from a saved page** — a real screenshot needs a browser Netlify functions do not have. | 2026-09-04 | (see next row) | | | **A background is not an ad, and an ad runs the set he picked.** Bryson: *"the image i select to use as a background image also got saved into the assets tab to be used for ads which i dont want only the finalized image or images should be saved"* and *"i dont want the current ad we have running for leads to use the storm related images I want them using the your leads arent sold to other roofers images"*. 🔴 **TWO BUGS, BOTH OF WHICH LOOKED LIKE FEATURES.** **One:** choosing a stock photo as a background **copies it into the library, and that copy is NOT optional** — the creative is drawn on a canvas and read back with `toBlob()`, and drawing a cross-origin image **taints the canvas so the read throws**, so the preview looks perfect and the download fails. Make a second angle later and the newest four are the new angle, and a running ad **silently switches onto it** — the storm-vs-leads report exactly, two angles in one library and the ad following the clock rather than the choice. Each save now stamps a **`group`** and a readable **`groupLabel`**, `campaignSetup.adCreativeGroup` records which set an ad runs, and the card gains a picker plus the line *"Only this set is used. The other designs in your library are left alone."* 🔴 **A deleted set falls back to the newest AND SAYS SO**, because silently swapping means he never learns his choice was lost. 🔴 **Files saved before groups existed do not merge** — each falls back to `single:<path>`, or two unrelated old creatives would become one set an ad runs both of. 🔴 **THREE pickers chose images independently** (launch card, live-ad creative swap, and the card's hint) and a fix to one would have left the others offering a bare background; all go through `adImages` now, and a check fails if any picker filters only videos again. 🔴 **TWO COPIES** (`netlify/lib/media-roles.mjs` and an inlined one in `index.html`, since the browser cannot import the lib): the suite **extracts the browser copy and runs every case against both**, so drift fails rather than ships. **16 checks; ten mutations, all caught**, including one that changed only the browser copy. Move the lead back to New to work it again and the status guard reopens, and a stranger gets a second report they never asked for once. The stamp does not reopen. 🔴 **THE WRITE RE-READS THE ROW FIRST**, because the whole payload goes back in one piece and writing the copy the screen loaded minutes ago could erase a "sent" stamp written since, which is precisely how a prospect gets emailed twice; it also refuses to overwrite a genuine send with a guess. **Second:** 🔴 **THE BANNER FIRED ON EVERY SINGLE LAUNCH, AND THE COMMENT ABOVE IT CLAIMED OTHERWISE.** It said it seeded the seen set on first load so existing leads would not toast. It seeded on the first RUN, which is not the same thing: `leads` starts as an empty array and the fetch has not returned, so the set was seeded with NOTHING, and the next run found every lead unseen and announced all of them. **The bug was not a missing seed, it was a seed taken against an empty list**, which is exactly what "it worked when I tested it with data already in memory" looks like. Fixed three ways: nothing is announced until the leads have genuinely loaded, the seen ids are remembered ON THE DEVICE (capped at 300, every read and write wrapped, because storage being refused should toast twice rather than crash), and only leads still sitting at New are announced at all. A first run on a new device seeds silently, so installing the app does not announce every lead he has ever had. 🔴 **AND THE SUITE ASSERTS THE OPPOSITE TOO**, because silencing a noisy notification is trivially achieved by breaking it: a genuinely new lead arriving while the app is open must still announce itself. **28 checks; eight mutations, all caught.** **Verified in a real browser**: silent on install, silent across two reopens, and a lead pushed into the running app still announced itself. KB: `lead-leak-delivery`, `lead-toast-repeat`. |
| 2026-09-04 | (see next row) | `rollback/20260905T004835Z` | `6cc6cf0` | **Shaun signed off the dry run, dropped the direct-post request, and asked for two things on the queue. One of them found a day-wide gap.** His reply: dry run received 1:31pm Pacific, status 200, signature verified, timestamp in window, payload validated, so the secret, the canonical form and the field names all lined up first try. He also withdrew the unsigned direct post (*"your reasoning holds"*) and confirmed the consent tick box reads exactly as filed. His asks: *"alert yourself if anything sits in it longer than a few minutes, and keep the lead_id stable across retries so my dedupe catches the replay"*. **The second was already true** and is now pinned from both directions: the id is a UUID stamped once at intake and stored on the lead, and the retry path hands the STORED lead back to the forwarder, so both wire formats replay the same value; a check now fails if it is ever generated at send time. 🔴 **THE FIRST FOUND A REAL GAP AND IT WAS A DAY WIDE.** The queue spoke up in exactly two situations: a wrong password (first failed sweep) and giving up (after the whole back-off ladder, which runs over 24 hours). **An ordinary outage in between was completely silent for that entire day**, and a lead is a person who has just asked to be called back. Now ten minutes, an amber alert naming the lead, how long it has waited and what their system said. Three things make it work and each is its own way to get it wrong: 🔴 **measured from the FIRST failure, not the last attempt**, because the last-attempt timestamp marches forward on every retry so a lead failing hourly would look one hour old forever and could never trip the check; 🔴 **a separate pass over EVERY lead**, because a lead sitting out a four-hour back-off is the most stuck thing in the queue and is exactly what the due-for-retry gate and the per-client cap both skip, so checking inside that loop would mean the longer a lead was stuck the less likely it was to be reported; and 🔴 **once per lead, never once per sweep**, or one stuck lead sends about a hundred identical alerts in a day. 🔴 **A MUTATION THAT RESET THE FIRST-FAILURE STAMP ESCAPED THE ENTIRE STUCK SUITE**, because every one of those cases built the timestamp by hand instead of letting the code produce it; the fix runs the real function three times and asserts the stamp survives. One older check had to be re-aimed: "a deliberate skip does not burn an attempt" asserted "nothing changed" as a stand-in for "nothing happened", which stopped being the same thing once a stuck lead legitimately gets stamped, so it now pins the try count, which is what it was always about. **72 checks; nine mutations, all caught after the eighth was re-aimed.** KB: `crm-retry-queue`. |
| 2026-09-04 | (see next row) | `rollback/20260905T010730Z` | `9e10d63` | **An em dash in the email subject line and the text message that go straight to a prospect.** Found while answering a plain question from Bryson about the Book a Call button (*"which email does it send from"*). 🔴 **FOUR PLACES, AND NO CHECK HAD EVER LOOKED AT ANY OF THEM.** The email SUBJECT ("BoldLine Media — let's book a quick call") and the text message ("it's Bryson from BoldLine Media — thanks for reaching out") each existed twice, once in the house account's Leads tab and once on the global Leads screen, because those two cards hold separate copies of the same code. 🔴 **THIS IS THE MOST DIRECT CLIENT-FACING COPY IN THE WHOLE OS AND IT LOOKED INTERNAL.** Everything else the dash rule guards is obviously outward facing: the portal, the marketing site, ad copy, client emails. These two strings sit inside a React component in Bryson's own dashboard, so they read as app code, and they are the exact words a prospect gets in their inbox and on their phone. **Where a string LIVES says nothing about who READS it**, and that is the lesson worth keeping. Fixed to a comma and two sentences. New check reads the drafts and the subject out of both cards and fails on an em dash, an en dash or a spaced hyphen; 🔴 it also **counts what it found and fails if fewer than six strings match**, because a pattern that silently stops matching is a check that passes while reading nothing, which is how this got here. **74 checks; four mutations, all caught**, including one that renamed a draft to prove the blindness guard works. |
